AWARD— PART II 

The Government of India, Ministry of Labour, Employment and Rehabilita- 
tion (Department of Labour and Employment) by order No. 29(II)/68-LR-IlI 
dated 27-7-1968 have referred to this Tribunal for adjudication an industrial dis- 
pute existing between the employers specified in the following schedule 1 hereto 
annexed and their workmen in respect of the matter specified In schedule H also 
hereto annexed: 

Schedule I 


1. M/s. V. M. Salgaoear & Bros. P*vt. Ltd, Vasco-dti-gama, 

2. M/s V, S. Denipo & Co. Pvt. Ltd., Panjlm. 

3. M/s J. H. Carvalho, Vasco-da-Gama. 

4. M/s. JoQo Vincent Fernandes, Vasco-da-Gama, 

5. M/s Narendra N, Kamat, Vasco-da-Gnma. 

6. M/s. General Superintend®nce Co. (India) Ltd-, Vasco-da-Gama. 

7. M/s Superintendence Co. of India Ltd., Vasco-da-Gama. 

8. M/s Itlab (Goa) Pvt. Ltd., Margao-goa. 

9. M/s Therepeutlcal Chemical Research Corpn., Margau, 

10. M/s Essen & Co. Behind Cloth BaEor, Margao. 

Schedule H 


“Whether the demand of the sampling workers for a weekly off with wages t.s 
justified? If not, to what relief are they entitled .' ' 

2. During the porlugue regime all the stevedoring and sampling work at the 
Mormugo.a Port was being performed by I.abour supplied by the Labour contrac- 
tors, This contract Labour system in re.speci of gang workers and 
winchmen had di.sappeared after the formation of ttic Dock Labour Board which 
regulated the .service conditions and v/age scales of Ihe two categories of workers. 
Ilovvever, the .sampling workers were not organized and the Government-approved 



SfiC. 3 (iij] TtlE GAZETTE OF INDIA: JANOART I, 1972/PAUSA 11 1893 5 


inspection agencies used to engage the sampling workers through the labour con- 
tractors. ine iabour contractors para the woricerB very row wages ana they were 
being oppressed and exploited by them, Shri D. A. Oeshpande, I’resident of the 
Uoa Docit Labour Union, organized this labour and the sampling workers joined the 
Goa Lock Laoour Union, ihis umon served the employcrai ot the sampling work- 
ers with a charter or dernamas regarding the revision oi scales of pay, dearness al- 
lowances. weekly oHs, etc., and subsequently had to resort to a strike Shri Desh 
pande had also raised a dispute with the Labour Commissioner and the strike con- 
tinued. Some of the employers issued charge-.sheets agamst Ihe striking employees 
lor an illegal strike and terminated their services. The matter was referred to the 
Conciliation Olheer but there was no settlement anti after the lailure report this 
dispute only m respect of the weekl-y' oil was referred to this Tribunal for adjudi- 
cation. 

3. The Goa Dock Labour Union by its .statement of claim has conteudeil that 
the export ol minerals and ores is being regulated In accordance with the provisions 
contained in the notification published in J?art II, sub-section (li) of section 3 of 
the Gazette of India Extraordinary dated the 20th December 106b and the sampl- 
ing of ores has got to be done by the inspection agencies approved by the Govern- 
ment of India and it has to be done at the port ol loading and no where else, 
i’here were aoout fitteen and sixteen mine owners and exporters and tnere were 
in all about 270 samplers with them. 'ihese workers have put in continuous 
service which ranged from 2 to 17 years without any break but still the employers 
considered them as temporary workers. It is alleged that the various Acts about 
labour such as payment of Wages Act, Minimum Wages Act, Workmen’s Co.iipeu- 
salion Act were simply inooperativc in the case of the sanipling worker.s and 
statutory welfare activities were not undertaken by the management and the 
wages were also far below subsistence level The union had made various 
demands but Government has only referred the demand about weekly ott as the 
employees were not getting weekly off even arter continuous work and they are 
entitled to gel this benefit which is a condition of sarvioe in respect of all sections 
of labour and the demand was justified. The union has in its statement of claim 
given details about the number of workers wages etc.. In respect of each employer- 

4. Out of the Ifi employers mentioned in schedule 1 Nos. 6 and 6 have settled 
the dispute between themselves and their workmen who were represented by the 
Goa Dock Labour Union presidied over by Shri Deshpande. That settlerrent 
though in favour of the workers was opposed by the Mormugao Vttaterfrorit 
Workers’ Union on the contention that there was no employer and employee rela- 
tionship but 1 have accepted the settlement and have passed award I'art 1 dated 
8th April 1969 and I shall in berief state the contentions raised by the other 
employers. 

5. Two employers M/s V. M. Salgaocar & Bros., Pvt. Ltd., and M/s V. S. 
Dempo & Co. Pvt. Ltd., have by their written statement opposed the reference 
on the technical contention that the appropriate Government for the purpo.se of 
the reference was the Administrator of Goa, Deman and Diu and the present 
dispute referred by the Central Government was incompetent and bad in law. 
It was further contended that there was no relationship of master and servant 
between the workmen and the company and this Court had no jurisdiction. 
Regarding the merits of the demand they have not stated anything but have 
merely contended that the sampling workers were rank casuals and they were 
working under the direct control and supervision ot the sampling agencies 
and they were not entided to any weekly oil. M/s V. S, Dempo & Co, Pvt. Ltd., 
have further contended that at the time of the reference there was no sampling work 
done within the limits of the Mormugoa Port and it was being done at the load- 
ing polnt.s and the Central Government was not the appropriate Government, 
It was further contended that the President of the Goa Dock Labour Union INTUC 
had sent a charter of demands on behalf of the .sampling workers for weekly off 
and the workers had also resorted to a .strike with effect from 27-2-1968. The 
strike ha^i taken place without an,y notice to the management and as the strike 
was illegal the management had decided to serve charge-sheets on the workers 
and after enquiry remove their names from the muster rolls and the workers 
were not entitled to any relief. 

6. Employer No. 3 M/s J, H. Carvalho has admitted that he was working as a 
sampling contractor for the laboratories In Goa and for carrying out thl.s work he 
wa.^i employing sampling boys depending upon the number of steamers on hand. 
The nature of the work was of a fluctuating character. lie could not employ any 
permanent hands in his small income except 3 to 4 mukadams who were supervis- 
ing the work of sampling boys. Within his small means it was impos.sible for him 
to Increase the wages and give them weekly paid holidays unless the rates of 
sampling were increased proportionately. He has further added that since 26-3-1968 
he was not doing the work. During the strike his crushing shed cum-offlce was set 
on fire. The entire premlse.s were gutted completely. He had lost everything 
Including the records pertaining to the business and since the date of the strike 
i.e. 27-3-1968 he had not done any sampling work and the workers were engaged 
by the .agencies directly and as the rates wor.? very low b,^ cniild not meet the de- 
mand and the workers were not entitled to anything. 

V Employer No. 4 Joa VIcents Fernandes, has by his written .statement dated 
19 (.i ;H68 contended that he was a small labour contractor. In 1965 after the quality 



6 laii tiAZBTTE OB INDIA; JANUARV J, lV72/l,'AtJ£A li. [Part ll- 


i;ontrol came into force the rates for Rampling and crushing work were reduced 
to almost one-third, the work also depenoed upon the whims of the exporters 
and the laboratories- Sometimes he did not get even one steamer for a whole 
month. Hia, income was insuthcaient and workers were not entitled to claim 
anything from him and the demand is not justified. 

8 Emp]oyer.s Nos. 7, to 10 are the inspection agencies carrying on the busine-ss 
o: ak-iayers- and sampling work. They have opposed the reference on the ground 
that there was no employer-employee reiationsulp between them and the workers 
and their name should be struck olf from the reierence. 

0. The Government had sent the copy of the order of reference only to the 
Goa Dock Labour Union as presumably that union represented the workmen of 
ihese employers- Subsequently Shri Gerald Pereira, President of the Mormugoa 
Water-front Workers' Umon, had pul in his appearance and as stated earlier had 
made an application opposing the settlement of the dispute between employers 
Nos, 5 and « and their workmen. Though the Waterfront Workers’ Union was 
not made a party Shrl Gerald Pereira was heard and accepting the settlement I 
passed an award Part 1. 

10. After this award unfortunately Shri D. A, Deshpande died and though the 
reference w'as adjourned irom lime to time nobody on benalt ot the Goa Dock 
Labour Union of Shri D. A. Deshpande attended the proceedings. On one occasion 
one Shri Shamba Shivram Bhonsle appeared on behalf of the Goa Dock Labour 
Union and filed an afhdavit which was not also sworn. An Advocate Shri V. N. 
Lawande also put in an appearasioe on behalf of Ihe union but subsequently he did 
not remain present. 

11. At the* last hearing one Shri H. V. Gondolkar who claimed to be the 
Assistant Secretary jjf the Goa Dock Labour union gave an application for adjourn- 
ment contending that the General Secretary of tne union was out of Goa. Shri 
Gondolkar further submitted that It should be adjourned to the next day when 
he would keep his witnesses present- Accordingly it was adjourned but the next 
day he did not appear nor were any witnesses present and the hearing was closed. 

17. In the reference order there was only one issue regarding the justiflablHty 
of the demand for weekly oil. The employers had resisted the reference mainly 
on the technical contentions such as jurisdiction, relationsnip of employer-emplo- 
yee etc. They had also opposed the reference only on the grounds of financial 
difficulties and the labour being casual. I have already observed that employers 
Nos. 5 and b had settled the dispute.. Though employer No. 2 had opposed the 
reference on various grounds they have also entered into a settlement with the 
union concerned. Although the parties have raised technical contentions they have 
not put forth any arguipents and the question is whether this Tribunal can 
adjudicate uhpon the reference and the reference is valid. 

13. Though the employers have contended that there was no sampling work 
being done with the limits of the Mormugoa Port and the sampling work was 
being done at the various loading points if Is Clear from th^ written statements 
of some of the employers themselves that the sampling work is to done at the port 
when the ship is being loaded, Shri J. H- Carvalho, employer No. 4 has in his 
written statement dated 5th February 1969 stated that he was working os a 
sampling contractor for various laboratories and the work he was undertaking 
involved the picking up of the samples from the steamer under loading to crusn 
the lumpy ore and send it to the laboratory for analysing. He has further stated 
that for doing this work he was engaging sampling boys depending upon the 
number of steamers on hand. This supports the contentions of the union In Its 
written statement that sampling of ores has got to be done by the inspection 
agency which was approved by the Government of India and it has to be done 
at the port of loading and nowhere else. It is not in dispute that quality control 
In respect of sampling of ores has been Introduced by the Government of India with 
effect rrom 1-4-1966. Employer No. 4 Joa 'Vlcents Fernandes has also stated that 
he was a sampling contractor at Mormugoa Harbour and this clearly shows that 
the sampling work has to be done In the port and In relation to an Industrial dis- 
pute concerning a major port the Central Government la the appropriate Govern- 
ment. The reference Is to be made only under section 10(1) (d) of the Act. It 
is not made under the rules framed. The Central Government has not also 
delegated their powers to the Union Territory and there Is no question of want 
of competency on the part of the Central Government to make the reference and 
the reference order passed by the Central Government is legal and the reference 
does not suffer from incompetency and this Tribunal has jurisdiction. 

14. As regards the other contention about the want of relationship between 
the employers and the workmen it is significant to note that the main contestants 
have cautiously used the words that on the date of the reference there was no 
relationship of master and servant. Emplo.yer No. 1 In his written statement 
dated 29-1-1068 paragraph 2(c) has stated that from 1-4-1966 to 2,6-3-1068 the 
Company used to supply such of the casual sampling workers as required by the 
sampling agencies and they stopped this contract of supplying the labour to the 
Sampling agencies from 26-3-1968 and it Is because of this circumstance they 
have contended that these persons were not their workmen. Similarly employer 
No, 2, in paragraph 4 of written statement dated 10-10-1968 has admitted that for the 



Sec. 3 (ii)] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA: JANUARY 1, 1972/PAUSA 11, 1893 7 


purpose of. doing the preshipraent samp! ng work the company had engaged 
the services of a reputed firm M/s Italao Pvi,., Ltd. and m order to assist the firm 
for doing the sampling work the company nad engaged some casual workers from 
time to time. They have further conlen..ed that because of the illegal strike they 
had charge-sheeted the workmen and removed their name.s from the muster. This 
clearly shows that they had engaged tho workman and the contention that the 
was no relationship of master and servant cannot accepted. 

15. It is further clear from tiie definition of “Workman" under the Act, that this 
term Includes any person who has been di.smisoed or discharged or retrenched in 
connection with or as a consequence of the dispute. The workers had demanded 
the revision of pay scales, weekly off and because of the demand they had gone 
on strike and the management removed them from service conteTidiiig that the 
strike was illegal and it .shall have to be held that the removal has in connection 
with the dispute and the employees are wo-kmen. Employers Nos. 3 and 4 admit- 
ted that they used to engage labour- Employer No. 7 and 9 oy letter.H dated 
28-6-1969 and 24-9-68 have stated that they had no labour employee.s working under 
them and they could not give any information, 9'hey were directed to file a written 
statement as per notice but they have not filed any written statement and it shall 
to be held that there isi relationship of master and servant between the parties anri 
the employees are workmen and this Tribunal has junsdictinn, 

16. I have already ob.served that the only issue referred for adjudication is about 
the ju.stiflabllltv of the demand for weekly off with wages. The employers have 
mostly opposed the reference on technical grounds. Considering the cunditiona of 
service of the other dock workeis and workers In other Industries, I do not think 
that the demand of the sampling workers lor weekly off with wages is unjustified. 
They are required to pick up the lumpy ore and crush It. It is hard work and after 
continuous work of six days the worker is morally entitled for rest. Employers 
Nos. 5 and 6 have already entered into a settlement and agreed to give a weekly 
off with wage.s-. Employer No, 2 has also agreed and it shall have to be held that 
the demand of the workers for weekly olT with wages Is justified and they are en- 
titled to a weekly off and hence my award accordingly, 

17. The .second part of the reference Is “If not to what relief are they entitled?”. 
1 have given my finding on the first part of the reference in the afflrmative and the 
question of the second part does not arise and the further question is whether the 
workers are entitled to the relief with retrospective effect. 

18. I have already dlscus.sed tthe circumstances under which the dispute was 
referred. The Dock Labour Union raised a dispute making variou.s demands. They 
had also given a strike notice and the workers had gone on strike from 23rd March, 
1968. Employer No. 1 has in hi.s affidavit stated that they have stopped the prac- 
tice of supplying sampling workers to the sampling agencies from 26th March, 
1968. Employer No, 3 has stated that since the strike of 27th March, 1068 he is 
thrown out of business and has di.scontinued to be a contractor on 8th April, 1908. 
Moreover the shed cum-offlce was set on fire and the entire premises were gutted 
completely. lie has produced photographs showing the position before and after 
the fire. It is further clear from th.n union’s statement of claim that it was having 
direct negotiations with the Inspection agende,? during the months of March' and 
April 1968 and a proposal to pool together all the sampling workers was mooted. 
It has further slated that the inspection agencies were convinced that the demand.s 
of the samplln" worker.s were lust, and legitimate and pccordingly they have been 
negotiating wi'h the workers directly. Employer No. B Italab (Goa) Pvt. Ltd., have 
given a purshia dated 20th Seotember, 1971 that they have been giving a weekly 
off to the labourers provided thev work continuously for n period of six days In a 
week. It Is nn< known as to froni what date the sysigm of supplying labour by the 
labour contradors ceased and the agencies started employing labour directly. 
Under the circumstances in my opinion there is no question of any retrospective 
effect and ths employees will be entitled lo n weekly off with wages from the 
date this award comes into operation. 

No order as to costs. 


(Sd.) A. T. Zambhe, 
Presiding Officer,

ORDERS

S.O. 6.— Whereas the Central Government is of opinion that an industrial dia-

Draft Notification 

In exercise of the powers conferred by sub-section (2) of section 26 of the 
Minimum Wages Act, 1M8 (11 of 1948), the Central Government hereby directs 
that for a further period of five years with effect from the 24th April, 1971, the 
provisions of sections 12, 13, 14 and 18 of the said Act shall not apply to railway 
servants who are on time scales of pay approved by the Central Government and 
governed by the provisions of Chapter VIA of the Indian Railways Act, 1890, 
and the Indian Railway Establishment Code and who are employed In any sche- 
duled employment on Railways,

PART III 

1 . Medical Facilities 

(a) Hospitals . — The three Central Hospitals one each at Dhanbad, Asansol and 
Manendragarh and twelve Regional Hospitals situated In different Coalfields con- 
tinued to function. The bed-strength of the Central Hospitals at Dhanbad, Asansol 
and Manendragarh is 300, 350 and 50 respectively, 

(b) Dispensaries.^One static allopathic dispensary at Mugma in Jharla Coal- 
field, two mobile medical units one each at Assam and Pench Valley Coalfields 
era 29 ayurvedic dispensaries were run during the period under report. For the 
manufacture of the genuine medicines required for the use at the ayurvedic 
dispensaries, the Ayurvedic Pharmaej-^ set up at Patherdih In the Jharia Ctoalileld, 
continued to render service. 



Sec. 3 (ii)] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA: JANUARY I, 1972/PAUSA 11, 1893 


(c) Family welfare, Maternity and C!"ld Welfare Centr-'s . — A Family Wcllrue 
Centre attached to each of the Regional Tlospifal;’ continued to function. Beside.:, 

8 svjch centres already established in the vuiouP coalfields were also functwnlng 
as independent unite, each under the charge of a qnaliflefi Lady Health Visitor. 
In addition, 58 Maternity and Child Welfare Centres were being run by the 
Asansol, Jharla anci Ha/aribagh Mines Boards of Ikalth for which the Coal Mines 
Labour Welfare Organisation continued to nay grant-in-aid towards their main- 
tenance. For the benefit ot the colliery worl'.''' s in the Chanda Coalfieds, the black 
attached to the Government Hospital, Chanda, con.inued to function. 

(d) Financial Assistance for imprnvinij Duye/isary Services . — With n view tn 
encouraging the colliery managements fur improving the standard of dispensary 
services at the c.allieries for the benefit of the colliery workers and their depen- 
dents, the scheme for the payment of g’-ant-in-.sid was continued and a sum of 
R.s. 12,51,756 was paid during the year. Further, in order to give incentive to 
colliery managements to provide new dispensaries or to improve the existing dis- 
pensary services for the benefit of the workers emnloyed by them, the Coal Mines 
Labour Welfare Organisation operated a scheme of financial assistance in the 
form of payment of inlere.st free loans equivalent to actual cost of construction 
or improvement ol existing building for the dispensaries including purchase of 
equipment stitajeci to the maximum of 16 times of the annual grant-in-aid. 

(e) Anti T. B. Measures: — Ee.sldes 262 beds provided by the Coal Mines Labour- 
Housing and General Welfare Fund, 61 beds remained reserved in the various 
T.B. Sanatoria for the treatment of colliery workers and their dependents suffering 
from T. B. The scheme of domiciliary T. B. treatment continued to function and 
payment of diet and subsistance allowances to the T. B. patients who were xmder- 
going treatment under this scheme also continued to be made according to 
schedule. A sum of Rs. 6,62,613.69 was paid diu-ing the period. For the reserva- 
tion of 01 beds, a sum of Rs. 1,21,955.36 was paid during tho year to the variou.s 
sanatorium authorities. 

(f) 'Faimlly ’PlanningTtServicts : — Free advice on'family planning continued to be given and 
contraceptive supplied free of cost at the Coal Mines Labour Housing and General Welfare Fund’, 
Hospitals and at the clinics openei for the purpose at some rural an 1 Urban areas. The scheme 
for the payment grant in aid to the colliery owners for the family planning work continued to 
be in force. 5 Static Family Planning Units and 3 Mobile Family Planning Units remained 
in operation. In addition, the Materinty and Child Welfare Centres numbering 58 run by the 
Jharla, Asansol and Hazaribag Mines Boards of Health in different coalfields for which the coal 
Mines Libsir velfirelOrgiaisatiori has been paying grant-in-aid, also provided family planning 
service for the benefit (.if the colliery workers and their dependents in those areas. 

(g) Rehabilitation : — A Rehabilitation cum-Physiotherapy Centre attached to each of 
the Central Hospitals at Dhanbad and Asansol, continued to function. The work of construction 
of the Rehabilitation Centre at Chhindwara ia in progress. The construction of the RehabiUta- 
tion-cum-Convalescent Homs at .Siihabarl was comnletsd ail necessary action for providing 
electric connection to the Centre was taken. 


fh) Other Activities Other important activities of the Coal Mines Labour Welfare Organ 
sation on the medical ,ind public health side were giving facilities for the treatment of Cancer, 
Leprosy and Mental Gases, running of blood bank at both the Central Hospitals at Dhanbad and 
Asansol, free supply of sp^^ctacles and dentures and Malaria and Filaria control operations.

S.O. 25. — Whereas it appears to the Central Government that the employer 
and the majority of the employees in relation to the establishment known as 
Messrs Nathan! Slitters & Decoilers Private Limited. Adjoining Vidya Vlhar 
Railway Station (West), Bombay-80 have agreed that the provisions of the Emp- 
loyees’ Provident Funds and Family Pension Fund Act, 1052 (19 of 1052), should 
be made applicable to the said establishment; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by sub-section (4) of 
section 1 of the said Act. the Central Government hereby applies the provisions 
of the said Act to the said establishment. 

This notification shall be deemed to have come into force on the first day of 
December, 1970.

S.O, 49.~Wherea3 Messrs Associated Instrument Manufacturers (India) Pri- 
vate Limited, B-3, Gillander House, 8, Netajl Subhas Road, Calcutta (hereinafter 
referred to as the said establishment), has applied for exemption under clause (a) 
of sub-section (1) of section 17 of the Employees' Provident Funds and Family 
Pension Fund Act, 1952' (19 of 3952); 

And whereas in thp opinion of the Central Government, the rules of the provi- 
dent fund of the said establishment with respect to the rates of contribution are 
not less favourable to the employees therein than those specified in section 6 of 
the said Act, and the employees are also in enjoyment of other Provident fund 
benefits which on the whole are not less favourable to the employees than the 
benefits provided under the said Act or under the Employees’ Provident Funds 
Scheme, 1952 (hereinafter referred to as the said Scheme) in relation to the 
employees in any other establishment of a similar character; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by clause (a) of sub- 
section (1) of section 17 of the said Act. and subject to the conditions specified in 
the Scheduled annexed hereto, the Central Government hereby exempts the said 
establi.shment from the operation of all the provisions of the said Scheme and in 
pursuance of sub-section (3i) of the said section 17, the Central Government 
hereby directs that, — 

(a) the employer in relation to the said establi.shment shall pav within 

fifteen days of the close of the month to the Employees’ Provident 
Fund, inspection charges at the rate of 0,09 per cent (zero point zero 
nine per cent) of the pay (baFic wape.s, dearness allowance, retaining 
allowance, if any. and cash value of food concession admissible there- 
on) for the time "being payable to the employees of the said establish- 
ment who would have become members under the said Scheme but 
for this exemption; 

(b) the said employer shall invest the provident fund contributions in .ac- 

cordance with the directions issued bv the Central Government from 
time to time. 

The Schkdui.e 

1 The employer shall submit such returns to the Regional Provident Firnd 
Commissioner as the Central Government may, from time to time, prescribe. 

2 The cmnlnver shall furnish to each employee an Annual Statement of Ac- 
count or Pa.ss Book, 

3 All expenses involved in the administration of the Fund including the main- 
ronance nf rwennnts. submission of aecouptc and returns, transfer of accumula 
tions n-,vment of imnectlon charges etc . shall be borne bv the emnlnver 

4 The emnlnver shall display on the Notice Board of t.He establishment a copy 
et tUe rules of the Fun6 as anprovod bv the aenronriate Government and at, ,nnd 
ivhen amended nloncwith a traralation of the salient points thereof in the larmu 
ave of the maioritv of the employees.

Fund (Rtaiutory Fund) or the Provident Fund of another exempted estahh'shment

is employed in his establishment, the employer shall immediately enrol him as 
a member of the Fund of the establishment, and accept the past accumulations 
in respect of such employee and credit to his account, 

0. The employer shall enhance the rate of provident fund contribution appro- 
priately if the rate of provident fund contributions for the class of establishments 
in which his establishment falls is enhanced under the Employees’ Provident. 
Funds and Family Pension Fund Act, 1952, so that the benefits under the provi- 
dent fund scheme of the establishment shall not become less favourable than the 
benefit provided under the Employees’ Provident Funds and Family Pension 
Fund Act, 1962. 

7. The establishment shall submit an audited balance sheet of its provident 
fund every year to the Regional Provident Fund Commissioner within 3 months 
of the close of the year. 

8. No amendment of the rules of the provident fund shall be made without 
the previous approval of the Central Provident Fund Commissioner. Where any 
amendment is likely to affect adversely the interests of the employees, the Cen- 
tral Provident Fund Commissioner shall, before giving his approval, give a rea-

Thf. Schedule 

1. The employer shall submit such returns to the Regional Provident Fund 
Commissioner as the Central Government may, from time to time, prescribe. 

2. The employer shall furni,sh to each employee an Annual Statement of 
Account or Pass Book.

lation!, payment of inspection chargetj etc., shall be borne by the employer.

respect of such employee and credit to his account.

priately if the rate of provident fund contribuUons for the class of establishments 
in which his e.stahlishment falls Is enhanced under the Emnloyeea’ Provident 
Fund.S and Family Pension Fund Act. 195?. so that the benefits under the provident 
fund scheme of the establishment shall not become le.ss favourable than the benefit 
provided under the Employees’ Provident Fimd.s and Family Pension Fund Act. 
1952. 

7. The establishment shall submit an audited balance sheet of ita provident 
fund every year to thn Regional Provident Fund Commissioner within 3 months 
of the close of the year. 

8. No amendment of the rules of tho provident fund shall be made without 
the previous annmval of the Central Provident Fund Commissioner. ’Where any 
amendment is likely to affect adversely the interests of the employees, the Central 
Provident Fund Commissioner shall, before vivina his apnroval, give a reasonable

Scheme, 1052 (hereinafter referred to as the said Scheme) in relation to the 
employees in any other establishment of a similar character; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by clause (a) of sub-section 
(1) of section 17 of the said Act, and subject to the conditions specified in the 
Schedule annexed hereto, the Central Government hereby exempts the said 
establishment from the operation of all the provisions of tne said Scheme and in 
pursuance of sub-section (3) of the said section 17, the Central Government hereby 
directs that: — ■ 

(a) the employer in relation to the said establishment shall pay within 
fifteen days of the close of the month to the Employees’ Provident 
Fund, inspection charges at the rate of 0.09 per cent (zero point zero 
nine per cent) of the pay (basic wages, dearness allowance, retaining 
allowance, if any, and cash value of food concession admissible 
thereon) for the time being payable to the employees of the said 
establishment who would have become members under the said 
Scheme but for this exemption; 

{b} the said employer shall invest the provident fund contributions in 
accordance with the directions issued by the Central Government 
from time to time. 

The Schedule 

1. The employer shall submit such returns to the Regional Provident Fund 
Commissioner as the Central Government may, from time to time, prescribe. 

2 . The employer shall furnish to each employee an Annual Statement of 
Account or Pass Book. 

3. All expenses involved in the administration of the Fund including the 
maintenance of accounts, submission of accounts and returns, transfer of accumu- 
lations, payment of inspection charges etc., shall be borne by the employer. 

4 . The employee shall display on the Notice Board of the establishment a copy 
of the rules of the Fund as approved by the appropriate Government and, as and 
when amended, alongwith a translation of the salient points thereof in the langu- 
age of the majority of the employees. 

6. Where an employee who is already member of the Employees’ Provident 
Fund (Statutory Fund) or the Provident Fund of another exempted establishment 
is employed in his establishment, the employer shall immediately enrol him as a 
meprl^r of the Fund of the establishment, and accept the past accumulations in 
respect of such employee and credit to his account. 

6. The employer shall enhance the rate of provident fund contribution appro-- 
priately if thg rate of provident fund contributions for the class of establishments 
m which his establishment falls is enhanced under the Employees’ Provident 
Funds and Family Pension Fund Act, 1952 so that the benefits under the provident 
fund scheme of the establishment shall not become less favourable than the benefit 
provided under the Employees’ Provident Funds and Family Pension Fund Act, 
1002 . ^ 

7. ’The establishment shall submit an audited balance sheet of its provident 
fund every year to the Regional Provident Fund Commissioner within 3 months 
Of the close of the year. 

8. No amendment of the rules of the provident fund shall be made without 
the previous approval of the Central Provident Fund Commissioner. Where any 
amendment ia likely to affect adversely the interests of the employees, the Central 
Provident Fund Commissioner shall, before giving his approval, give a reasonable 
opportunity to the employees to explain their point of view.

S.O. 69. — Whereas the Central Government was satisfied that Messrs Bhakra 
Cotton and General Mills (Private) Limited was situated in Sirsa area which was 
a sparse area (that is, an area whose Insurahle population was less than 600) In 
the district of HIssar In the State of Haryana; 

And, whereas by virtue of Its location In a sparse area the aforesaid factory 
was granted exemption from the payment of the employe''s’ special contribution 
under section 73F of the Employees’ State Insurance Act, 1948 (34 of 1948) untU 
enforcement of the provisions of Chapter V of the Act in that Area by the Central 
Government in the notification of the Government of India in the late Department 
of Social Security No. S.O. 3230, dated the 25th September, 1965; 

And, whereas the Central Government Is satisfied that the insurable population 
of the Sirsa area In the District of Hissar in the State of Haryana has now exceeded 
500, and it is no longer a sparse area; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by section 73F of the 
ICmployees’ State Insurance Act. 1948 (34 of 1948), the Central Government hereby 
makes the following further amendment in the said notification, namely: — 

In the Schedule to the said notification against Serial No. 6 the entry "Sirsa" 
in column 3 and the corresponding entry in column 4 shall be omitted.

S.O, 79. — In exercise of the powers conferred by section 88 of the Employees’ 
State Insurance Act, 1948 (34 of 1948), and in supersession of the notlflcafions ol 
the Government ol India in tire Ministry ol Labour, Employment and Rehabilitation 
(Department of Labour and Employment No. S.O. 2969, dated the 15th July, 1969 
Shram Aur Punarwas Mantralaya (Shram Aur Rozgar Vibhag) No. S.O, 2230, dated 
the 27th May 1971 and Ministry of Labour and Rehabihtatlon (Department of 
Labour and Employment No. S.O. 3366 dated the 18th August, 1971, the Central 
Government hereby exempts the monthly rated employees of the Fertilizer Cor- 
poration of India Limited, Trombay Division, Bombay Irom the operation of the 
said Act for the period from the 29th May, 1968 to the 26th July, 1972. 

2. The above exemption is subject to the following conditions, namely; — 

(I) The aforesaid factory shall maintain a register showing the names and 

designations ol the employees; and 

(II) that, notwithstanding this exemption, the employees shaU. continue to 

receive such benefits under the said Act to which they might have 
qualified on the basis ol contributions paid before the dates ol ex- 
emption.

of Messrs Lodna Colliery Company (1920) Limited, Post Office Kalipahari, District

AWARD 

The Central Government, having received the arbitration agreement dated 23rd 
September, 1971 between ine employer in relation to Srlpur Colliery .-'f M/s. Lodna 
Colliery Co. (1920) Ltd,, P. O. Kalipahari, Distt. Budwun (hereinafter referred to 
as tiie managemeni) and their workmen represented by the ColJier.y Mazdoor Cong- 
res.s (H„ M. S.) Bengar Hotel, P. O. Asansol, Distt, Burdwan (Hereinafter referred 
to as the union) in pursuance of sub-section (1) of seciion IDA id the Indusirlal 
Dl.sputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947) referring tho industrial dispute between them, the 
spccillc matters hi dispuie being detailed berow, to my arbitration, and the Central 
Government being of the opinion that the industrial dispute reierred to above 
existed between the management and their workmen, ordered publication of the 
said arbitration agieement in the Gazette of India, Part II, Section 3, sub-section 
(ii) under Its order bearing No. L/1913/,12/71-LRI1, dated 14th October, 1971. 

Specific matters in Dispute: 

"(1) Whether the action of the management of Srlpur Colliery of M/s. Lodna 
Colliery Co. (1920) Ltd., P.O. Kalipahari, Distt. Burdwan In refusing 
employment to Shri Ramgarib Koiry, Loader with etfect from ]2th 
February, 1970 is justilied? If not, to what redef is the workman 
concerned entitled? 

(il) Whether the action of the management of Srlpur Colliery pf M/s. Lodna 
Colliery Co. (1920) Ltd. P.O. Kalipahari, Distt. Burdwan in dismissing 
Shii Hurbans Kurnii, Underground Loader with etfcct from 12th June, 
1970 i.s justified? If not, to what reuet is the workman concerned 
entitled?” 

2. The union was requested to submit its writteix statement under my letter 
No. E.l/ARB/ 71, dated 28th October, 1971 endorsing a copy thereof to the manage- 
ment, within ten days from the date of receipt of my letter referred 
to above. Simultaneously, the management was requested to submit rejoinder to 
the written statement of the union. If any, received by tliem within seven days 
from the date of receipt of the written statement of the union endorsing its copy 
to the union under registered post with A/D under intimation to me. The union 
submitted Its written statement on 6th November, 1971 which was received in this 
office on 9th November, 1971. The management submitted rejoinder on 13th 
November, 1971 which was received In this office on IHth November, 1971. There- 
after the matter came up foi- hearing on 10th December, 1971 in the office of the 
Regional Labour Commissioner iC), Asansol. On that date Shri B. W. Bljapurkar, 
Dy. Chief Personnel Officer was present on behalf of the management and Shri 
Joyanta Podder, Joint Secretary, Colliery Mazdoor Congress (HMS), Asansol and 
Shri Rarn Bhajan Nunia, Treasurer, Sripur CoUJeiy Branch Conimlttae of the 



Sec. 3 (ii)] THE GAEBITE OF INDIA: JANUARY I, 1972/PAUSA li, 1S93 67 


Colliery Mazdoor Congress (HMS) were present on behalf of the workmen. On 
thnt date the parties filed a ioint petition of compromise statinft therein that the 
instant industiial dls'iiUe has been amicably settled between them on the terms 
detailed in the cumproniLse petition dated 10th December, 1971 and that 1 might 
accept the terms of setllement incorporated in the .foint petition of compiomisc 
referred to above and pass my award accordingly. The terms of the settlement incor- 
porated in the joint petition of compromise dated lOlh December, 1071 were duly 
verified and brought on record. The terms of the settlement incorporated in the 
joint petition of compromise dated 10th December. 1971 appear to me to be fair, 
reasonable and in the interest of the workmen concerned, and as such I accept the 
same. Accordingly, I make my arbitration award in terms of the jolht petition of 
compromise dated 10th December, 1971. The joint petition of compromise is 
annerred hereto and mn'iG part of the award. It ia submitted to the Central Gov- 
ernment under Section 10A(4) of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947. 

(Sd.) K. Sharan,

The representative of Ihe employer and the union named above submit as 
under : —

tarily resigned from his services of M/s. Lodna Collierv Co. (1920) 
Ltd., on nth February, 1970 and as such the union drops his case. 

(II) (a) It is agreed that the employer named above shall provide fresh 

employment to Shri Harbans Kurmi to wmrk as permanent underground 
Loader in Sripur Colliery with effect from 20th December, 1971. 

(ill (b) The union agrees that Shri Harbans Kumarl Is not entitled to any 
‘.voges or ebowances from 4th March, 1970 to 19th December, 1971.

may be pleased to accepi the terms of the settlement and give his award in terms 
of the settlement ref'^rred to In para No. 1 above.

New Delhi, the 10th November 1971 

S.O, 122. — Whereas the Central Government is of opinion that an industrial 
dispute exists between the employers in relation to the management of Messrs 
Therapeutics Chemical Research Corporation, Margao, Goa and their workmen, in 
respect of the matters specified In the Schedule hereto annexed; 

And whereas, the Central Government considers it desirable to refer the said 
dispute for adjudication; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by clause d) of sub-section 
(1) of section 10 of the Industrial Disputes Act; 1947 (14 of 1947;, the Central 
Government hereby refers the said dispute for adjudication to the Central Govern- 
ment Industrial Tribunal, No. 2. Bombay, constituted under section 7A of the said 
Act. 



Sec. 3 (ii)] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA: JANUARY 1, 1972/PAUSA 11, 1893 91 


Schedule 

1. (aj Whether the management of Messrs Therapeutic Chemical Research 

Corporation Margao is justified in not granting to their pre sampling 
workmen the benefits of dearness allowance and interim relief recom- 
mended by the Central Wage Board for Port and Dock Workers at 
Major Ports and as accepted by the Government of India in Resolution 
No. WB-2](14)/65, dated 27th April, 1965 and No. WB-21(14)/60, dated 
the 19th October. 1968? 

(b) If not, to what relief are the workmen entitled? 

2. (a) Whether the management of Messrs Therapeutic Chemical Research Cor- 

poration, Margao is justified In not granting to thglr ore sampling 
workmen the benefits of final recommendation relating to wage struc- 
ture, dearness allowance, house rent allowance and compensatory 
allowance of the Central Wage Board for Port & Dock Workers at 
Major Ports with effect from th'e 1st .January. 1069 as accepted by the 
Government of India in Resolution No. WB-21(7)/69, dated the 28th 
March, 1970? 

(b) If not, to what relief are the workmen entitled? 

3. (a) Whether the demands of the ore sampling workmen in respect of the 

following items are justified? 

(I) Paiment of overtime at double the normal rates of pay for work on 

Sundays, other port holidays and for work on normal days, 

(II) Grant of compensatory off for work on weekly holidays within fixed 

period; 

(III) Grant of 30 days’ privilege leave, 10 days’ casual and 7 days’ sick 

leave; 

(b) If so, to what relief are the workmen entitled? 

[No. 77/1/70-P&D.] 

0. P. TALWAR, Dy. Secy.

S.O. 123, — ^Whereas an application has been made under section 19(ib) of the 
Payment of Bonus Act, 1905 by Contractors of Messrs Salem Magnesite Pvt., Ltd,, 




94 the gazette of INDIA: JANUARY I, I 972 /PAUSA II. 1893 [Part H— 


(employer) in relation to their establishments mentioned In the Schedule below 
for extension of the period for the payment of bonus to their employees for the 
accounting year ending on 31st December, 1970, 

And whereas being satisfied that there are sufficient reasons to extend the time 
I have, in exercise of the powers conferred on me by the proviso to clause (b) of 
Section 19 of the said Act, read with the notification of the Government of India 
In the Ministry of Labour and Employment No. WB. 20(42)/66 dated the 28th, 
August, 1965, passed order on 25th September, 1971 extending the period for pay- 
ment of the said bonus by the said employer by 1 month 20 days (l.e, up to 20th 
October, 1971), from the last date for payment of bonus under clause (b) of 
Section 19 of the Act. 

Now this Is published for information of the employer and aU the employees 
of the said establishment.

S.O. 1S4.— In exercise of the powers conferred on the Central Government 
under Section 34(1) of the Displaced persons (Compensation & Rehabilitation), 
Act, 1954, the Central Government hereby directs that any power exercisable by 
it under Sections 3, 16, 28, 33, & 34(1) of the said Act, shaU be exercisable also 
by the State Government of Maharashtra in respect of the lands and properties 
within the State and forming part of the ‘Compensation Pool subject to the 
condition that the powers under Section 18 shall not be exercised in respect of 
any matter affecting the payment of Compensation and Rehabilitation grant 
to any displaced person either by way of transfer of any pool property or other- 
wise and that the State Government ahall not under these delegated powers 
take any step’ for the disposal of compensation pool excepting the lands and 
properties of compensation pool within the State of Maharashtra as were not 
disposed of before Ist August, 1071.

S.O, 142 — Whereas the Election Commission is satisfied that Shri H. P. Gana- 
pathi Prabhu, Land Holder, Harlharapura Post, Koppa Taluk, Chikmagalur Dis- 
trict (Mysore) a contesting candidate for the general election to th® House of 
the People from 19-Chikmagalur constituency, held in March, 1971 has failed to 
lodge an account of his election expenses as required by the Representartion of the 
People Act, 1951, and tho Rules made thereunder; 

And, whereas, the said candidate even after due notices has not given any 
reason or explanation for tho failure and the Election Commission la) satisfied that 
he has no good reason or justification for the failure. 

Now, therefore, In pursuance of section lOA of the said Act, the Election Com- 
mission hereby declares the said Shri H. P. Ganapathl Prabhu to be disqualified for 
being cho.sen as. and for being, a member of either House of Parliament or of the 
Legislative Assembly or Legislative Council of a State for a period of three years 
from the date of this order.

OFFICE OF THE COMMISSION OF INQUIRY 

New Delhi, the 7th December 1071 

S.O. 154 . — Whereas by the Cabinet Secretariat, Department of Personnel Noti- 
fication No. S.O, .3883, dated October 16, 1071, the Central Government has ap- 
polnted a Commission of Inquiry to inquire into and report within three months 
on the allegatioifi made against certain former Ministers of the State of Punjab by — 

(i) Shri Trllochan Singh Rlyastl In a memorandum dated the 20th July, 
1071, presented to the President of India, and 
(il) Shri Satyapal Dang, in his letters aated the 6th July, 1971 anid Slst 
July, 1971. addressed to the Governor of the State of Punjab. 




tiEc. 3 (u)] Tilt, Gazette of j>ide\; JAF^UAliv i, 1972/FAOEiA 11, tsoi nj 


Now, therefor®, this Notification ia issued, oy and under the order oi the Com- 
mifision inviting all pereons acquainted with the subject rnatter of the inquiry to 
furnish to vhc Commission attldavlts containing a statement of facts set out in 
several paragraphs relating to such of the charges contained In the aforesaid 
memorandum and letters as to winch the deponent Is competent to depose.^ Such 
atlidavits must be strictly in accordance with the provisions of Order XIX of 
the Code of Civil Procedure. They should be confined to the charges only and 
the statements of facts to be set forth in the affiidavita must be expressed to be 
related to particular Items of charges- The attldavlis must be properly verified in 

the lollowmg manner, namely, “that the statements in paragraph and 

...of the foregoing affidavit are true to rny knowledge’’ and in case the 

statements are derived from inioimation received, "that thg statements in para- 
graphs and are based on information received by me from 

(naming the Informant) resident at and believed by me to be 

teue.’’ If the information has been received from an official of the Punjab Gov- 
®rnment and the deponent does' not desire to disclose his name, the verification 

should toe that "the statements contained in paragraphs and 

are derived from official file No (identltylng the file).” The deponent 

should also furnish to the Commission along with the affidavit a list of the docu- 
ments, If any, on which he proposes to rely and forward to the Commission the 
origlhals or true copies of such of the documents as may be In his poBsession er 
power and shall slate the name and address of the person from whom the re- 
maining documents be obtained. The original affidavits must be filed on or before 
December 20, 1971 at the Office of the Commission, at Punjab House, Lytton Road, 
New Delhi, on any working day between the hours of 10-20 a.m. and 1-00 p.m. 
The deponent should also supply sufficient number of copies for distribution to 
the memorialists and the ex-Minlster concerned. Inspection may be had of the 
copy of the aforesaid memorandum and letters filed before the President of India 
and Governor of Punjab at the said office of the Commission on any working day 
between the hourr of 10-30 a.m, and 1-00 p.m, Alfidavtts not verified in the manner 
indicated above or not filed within the date and time ppedfled above will not be 
taken into consideration by the Commission. 

[No- 1/2/71-COM.] 
PARMANAND, 

Secretary to th* Commis.sion of Inquiry,

(Department of Revenae and Insnrance) 

Insurance 

New Delhi, the 16th November 1971 

8.0. 187. — The following draft of rules further to amend the Insurance Rules, 
1939, which the Central Government proposes to make in exercise of the powers 
conferred by section 64UB and section 114 of the Insurance Act, 1938 (4 of 1938), 
is published, as required by sub-section (1) of the said section 114, for the infor- 
mation ol all persons likely to be affected thereby, and notice is hereby given that 
the said draft will be taken Into consideration on or after 31st December, 1971. 

2. Any ob.lecLions or suggestions which may be received from any person with 
respect to the draft on or before the date specified above will ibe considered by 
the Central Government. 


Draft Rules 

1. These rules may be called the Insurance (Second Amendment) Rules, 1971. 

2. In the Insurance Rules, 1939 (hereinafter referred to as the said rules), riile 
40 shall be omitted. 

3. For rule 60 of the said rules, the following rule shall be substituted, namely; — 
“60. Term of Office . — Any person nominated as a member of the Consultative 

Committee constituted under sub-section (1) of section llOG of the Act 
(hereafter referred to in rules 61 to 66 as the Commltteel shall hold 
office for a period not exceeding three years or such further periods 
not exceeding thtee years at a time as the Central Government may 
apacjfy." 




152 the gazette of INDIA: JANUARY 1. iy72/PAUSA 11, 1893 [Part H— 


4. In the said rules, in rule 61, lor sub-rule (1), the following sub-rule shall be 
substituted, namely: — 

"(1) Any member of the Committee may resign his membership of the Com- 
mittee by notice In writing addressed to the Secretary to the Govern- 
ment of India, Ministry of Finance (Department of Revenue and 
Insurance) to that elfect and such resignation shall take effect from 
the date on which it is accepted by the Central Government or on the 
expiry of a period of thirty days from the date of its receipt by the 
Central Government, whichever Is earlier.”. 

6. In the said rules, In Schedule II, for regulation 13, the following regulation 
shall be substituted, namely: — 

"13. Disputes. — If any doubt or dispute arises regarding the Interpretation of 
the provisions of these regulations or regarding the validity of any 
election held under these regulations, it shall be reffered to the 
Central Government for decision.".

Where an Income-tax Circle, Ward, or District or part thereof stands trans- 
ferred by this Notification from one Range to another Range, appeals arising out 
of assessments made in the Income-tax Circle, Ward or District or part thereof 
and pending immediately before the date of this Notification before the Appellate 
Assistant Commissioner of Income-tax of the Range from which the Income-tax 
Circle, Ward or District or part thereof is transferred shall, from the date this 
Notification shall take efifect, be transferred to and dealt with by the Appellate 
Assistant Commissioner of the Range to whom the said Circle, Ward or District 
or part thereof is transferred. 

This Notification shall take effect from 1st November, 1971. 

Explanatory No*te 

The amendments have become necessary on account of the re-allocation of 
jurisdiction of the Appellate Assistant Commissioners of Income-tax in the char- 
ges of Andhra Pradesh.

Circle, at Bolangir and con.sequent reorganisation of Tltlagarh Circle also.

clariflcatory).

S.O. 201. — In exercise of the powers conferred by Section 10 of the Customs 
Act, 1962 (52 of 1962), the Collector of Customs and Central Excise, Ahmedabad, 
hereby declares OKHA BEYT (SANKHODtl/AR BE'l^) situated on the southern 
coast and NA'VINAL POINT (a place in the port limit of Mundra) on the Northern 
coast of the Gulf of Kutch, as Boarding Stations and orders that all sailing vessels 
entering or leaving the ‘Gulf of Kutch end passing through the port limits of the 
ports OKHA BEYT (SANKHODWAR BEYT) of MUND(RA shall call at either of the 
above referred boarding station.s for the purpose of boarding of, or disembarka- 
tion from vessels by officers of Customs.

l.e. from the mld-nlght of 31st March, 1971/ Ist April, 1971.

New Delhi, the 3rd December 1971 

S.O. 210.— Whereas the Textiles Committee has established" standard specifi- 
cations' for the purposes of export for the material as defined In clause (d) lOf 
regulation 2 of the Polyester-Cotton and Polyester-Viscose Blended Fabrics Ins- 
pection .EegulatlonB,-,1971. 

And. whereas, on the recommendation made to it In this behalf by the Textiles, 
Committee, the Central Government is of opinion that the material which doea not 
confirm to the standard specifications established by the said Committee should 
not be exported; 

Now therefore, In exercise of the powers conferred by sub-section (1) of 
section ’l7 of the Textiles Committee Act, 1963 (4l of 1963). the Central Govern- 
ment hereby prohibits the export, from India to any foreign country, of the mate- 
rial defined in clause (d) of regulation 2 of the Polyester-Cotton and Polyester- 
Viscose Blended Fabrics Inspection Regulations. 1971, unless such material Is 
covered by a ce-'’flcate issued by the officer authorised by the Textiles Commit- 
tee in this behalf under regulation 11 of the said regulations. 

Provided that the above prohibition sihall not auply to the export of low stand- 
ard material which does not conform to the minimum standard required for the 
Issue of a certificate under regulation 11 of the said regulations, If an order Is 
received from abroad for the supply of such low standard material and If. the 



l68 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA: JANUARY I. rj’I./l'AUSA 1 . 1893 [Part IJ~ 


officer authorised by the Textiles Committee in this behalf is satisfied about the 
bonafldes of such order, and the export of such material is authorised by him. 

2_ This Notification shall come into force on the 15th February, 1972. 

[F. No. 18/46/69-Tex-A.l 
H. K. BANSAL, Dy. Secy,

S.O. 213. — M/s. Prem Export and Import Corporation, F-2/22A, Model Town, 
Delhi-9 were granted an Established Importers licence No. P/E/0l83fi97/C/XX/ 
36/D/31-32 dated 1.3th September 1970 for Rs. 1,000/- for Import of Motor Vehic'.e 
Parts as per Appendix 26 of the Red Book for A,M.71 period. They have applied 
for the Duplicate Cu.stom.s purposes and Exchange Control copy of the .said licence 
on the ground that the original had been lost or misplaced. It Is, further, stated 
by the firm that the original Cuslom.s purpose copy of the licence is not registered 
with any Custom House and hence has not been utilised. 

In auport of Ihhs declaration, the applicant has filed an affidavit duly attested by 
the Notary stating that the original Customs purpose and Exchange Control copy of 
the licence have been lost or misplaced. 

I am satisfied that the original Customs and Exchange Control copy of the said 
licence have been lost and direct that a Duplicate Customs purpose copy and 
Exchange Control copy should be issued to the applicant. The original Customs 
purpose and Exchange Control copy of the licence are cancelled.

S.O. 217.— The following licences were issued to M/s. Hindustani Udyog, 139, 
Dayanand Path, Meerut (Canttj): — 

(1) P/S/1685180 dt. 27th February, 1971 for the import of Copper Zinc, 

(2) P/S/ 1685181 dt. 27th February, 1971 Lead and Nickel. 



Sec. 3 (ii)] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA; JANUARY 1. l97i/fAUSA ll, 1893 


Thereafter a show cause No. Enf. II(130)/1970/KAN/400 dt. the 16th April, 
1971 was Issued asking them to show cause within 15 (fifteen) days as to why the 
item Copper should not be deleted, from their said Ecences for the reason that it 
wa.s Included in them In advertantly, in terms of clause 7 of Import (Control) 
Order, 1955, as amended. 

In response to the aforesaid show cause notice no reply has been received from 
M/s, Hindustani Udyog, Meerut Cantt. and the .stipulated period of 15 days Is over. 

The undersigned has carefully examined the case and has come to the con- 
clusion that the party has no defence to urge and has avoided a reply. 

Having regard to what has been stated In the preceeding paragraph the under- 
signed is satisfied that the item in question should be deleted or otherwise rendered 
ineffective. Therefore, the undersigned in exercise of powers vested in him under 
clause 7 of the Import (Control) Order, 1955 hereby deletes the item Copper from 
the licences mentioned above issued in favour of Mys. Hindustani Udyog, 139, 
Dayanand Path, Meerut Cantt.

ORDER 

Kanpur, the 19th June 1971 

S.O, 219. — ^The following Import licences were issued to M/s. U.P. Industries, 
Multanipura, Modinagar (U.P.): — 

(1) P/S/1655010 dt. 30th September,. 1970 for Rs. 6000/-, 1 for the import of Ra* 
(ii) P/S/1666011 dt. 30th September, 1970 for Rs. 6000/- I Bearings (non banned 

}-&-non restricted types 
I Taper RoHar Bearings 
j permissible types, etc.) 

2. Thereafter a show cause notice No. Enf.II(99)/1970/K.N. dated the 5th May, 
1971 was issued asking them to show cause within 15 (fifteen) days of the receipt 
of notice as to why the said licences in their favour should not be cancelled on 
the ground that their factory had closed down and no manutacturing activities 
were being carried on there. 

3. The said show cause notice has been returned by the postal authorities with 
the remarks that the receipient had gone out an unknown address & it was known 
when he will return. 

4. The undersigned has carefully considered the case and has come to the con- 
clusion that M/s. U.P, Industries, Maltanipura, Modinagar, Uttar Pradesh, have 



Sec. 3 (ii)] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA; JANUARY 1, 1972/PAUSA II, 1893 lyj 


closed down and no manufacturing activities are carried by them. The afore- 
said licences will, therefore, not serve the purpose for which they were issued/ 
avoided a reply and have no defence to urge. That their factory. 

Having regard to what has been stated in the preceding paragraph the under- 
signed is satisfied that the licences in question should be cancelled or otherwise 
rendered ineffective. Therefore, the undersigned in exercise of powers vested 
in him under clause 9, sub-clause (cc) of the Imports (Control) Order, 1955 hereby 
cancels the licences No. P/S/1655010, dt. 30th September 1970 & P/S/1655011, dt. 
30th September, 1970 for Rs. 5000 and Rs. 5000 respectively Issued in favour of 
M/s. U.P, Industries, Multanipura, Modinagar (U.P.). 

[No, ENF.II(99) /1970/KAN,]

S.O. 220. — The following import licences were issued to M/s. A. E. Engineering 
Co, near Modi College, Modinagar (U.P.). — 

^i) P/S/1655980 dt. 29th September, 1970 for Rs. 4000/- for the import of 
(li) P/S/1655987 dt. 29th September, 1970 for Rs. 4000 /-ball bearings non ban- 
ned & non restricted 
types. 

2, Thereafter a show cause notice No. Enf.II(99) /1970/KAN dated the 5th May, 
1971 was Issued asking them to show cause within 15 (fifteen) day.s of the receipt 
of notice as to why the said licences in their favour should not be cancelled on 
the ground that their factory had closed down and no manufacturing activities 
were being carried on there. 



176 Tf^t' GAZETTE OF INDIA; JANUARY 1 !97j',/|'/vi Sa ll F-J3 [Pari II-- 


3. The said show cause notice has been returned by the postal authorities with 
the remarks that the firm had closed down. 

4. The undersigned has carefuUy considered the case and has come to the con- 
clusion that M/s. A. E. Engineering Co. near Modi College, Modinagar (U.P.) have 
closed down and no manufacturing activities are being carried by them. The 
aforesaid licences will, therefore, not serve the purpose for which they were issued. 

5. Having regard to what has been stated In the preceding paragraph the under- 
signed Is satisfied that the licences In question should be cancelled or otherwise 
rendered ineffective. Therefore, the undersigned In exercise of powers vested 
in him under 9, sub-clause (cc) of the Imports (Control) Order, 1955 hereby 
cancels the licences No. P/S/1655986 dt. 29th September, 1970 & P/S/1655987 dt. 
29th September, 1970 for Rs. 4000 and Rs. 4000 respectively Issued In favour of 
M/s. A. E. Engineering Co. near Modi College, Modinagar (U.P.). 

[No. ENF.II(90) /1970/KAN.] 
P. C. BHATNAGAR,

In transit and hereby cancel the same.

MINISTRY OF HOME AFFAIRS 

Nt'ir Delhi, the 27th November 1971 

S.O. 343. — In exercise of tlm powcis conferred by section 18 of the Central 
Reserve Police Force Act, 1949 (86 tT 1949), the Central Government hereby makes 
the following rules further to amend Ihc Central Reserve Police Force Rules, 1955, 
namely:-- 

1, (1) These rules may be called the Central Reserve Police Force (Second 
Amendment) Rule.s, 1971. 

(2) They shall come into force on the date of their publication in the official 
Gazette. 

2. In the Central Re.serve Police Force Rules. 1956, for clause (d) of rule 10, 
the following clause shall be substituted, namely:— 

(d) (1) The Central Government may, having regard to the special circums- 
tances of any cose, permit any member of the Force appointed under section 
4 of the Act to resign from the Force before the attainment of the age of retire- 
ment or before putting in such number of years of service as may be necessary 
under the rules to be eligible for retirement; 

Provided that while granting .such permission, the Central Government may: 

(1) require the member of the Force to refund to the Government such 
amount as would constitute the cost of training given to that 
membci' of the Force; or 



194 GAZETTE OF INDIA; JANUARY 1. 1972/PAUSA 11, 1893 [Part U 


(ii) make such reduction in the pension or other retiring benefits of 
that member of the Force, if so eligible, as that Government may 
consider to be 3ust and proper in the circumstances. 

(2) The Central Government may accept the resignation under this clause with 
effect from such date os it may consider expedient: 

Provided that it shall not be later than tree months from the date of 
receipt o£ such resignation. 

(3) The Central Government may refuse to permit an officer to resign: 

(a) if an emergency has been declared in the country either due to internal 

disturbances or external aggression; or 

(b) if it considers it to be inexpedient so to do in the interests of the dis- 
cipline of the Force; or 

(c) if the officer has undertaken to serve for a specific period and such 
period ha.s not expired. 

(4) The provisions of this clause shall apply also in relation to subordinate 
officers and other members of the Force appointed by the Commandant as they 
apply in relation to any member of the Force appointed under section 4 of the 
Act and the powers vested in the Central Government under items (1), (21 and 
(3) shall be exercised In the case of a subordinate officer by a Deputy Inspector 
General of Police and in the case of other members of the Force by a Comman- 
dant.

MINISTRY OF EDUCATION AND SOCIAL WELFARE 

New Delhi, the 26th November 1971 

8.0. 270, — ^Whereas the Central Governinent ia of opinion that the ancient 
monument specified In the Schedule attached hereto is of national importance. 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by ,siub-aection (I) of section 
4 of the Ancient Monuments and Archaeological Sites and Remains Act, 1958 (24 
of 1958), the Central Government hereby gives notice of its intention to declare 
the said ancient monument to be of national Importance. 

Any objection made within two months from the date of publication of this noti- 
flcoitlon in the official Gazette by any person Interested in the said ancient monu- 
ment will be considered by the Central Government.

Sec. 3 (ii)j THE GAZETTE OF INDIA; JANUARY I, 1972/PAUSA II, 1893 289 


(Department of Education) 

New Delhi, the 10th December 1971 

S.O, 2717. — The Visitor having nominated Professor S. Nurul Hasan, Minister 
of State in the Ministry of Education and Social Welfare, to be a member of 
the Council in pursuance of clause (j) of sub-section (2) of section 31 of the 
Institutes of Tecnnology Act, 1961 (59 of 1961), vice Dr. H. N. Sethna, the Cen- 
tral Government hereby makes the following further amendment in the notifica- 
tion of the Government of India in the late Ministry of Scientific Research and 
Cultural Affairs No. S.O. 1618, dated the 9th May, 1962, namely: — 

In the said notification, under the heading “IV. Nominees of the Visitor,” in 
item (j), for the existing entry (iv), the following entry shall be substituted, 
namely: — 

“(iv) Professor S. Nurul Hasan, Minister of State, Ministry of Education 
and Social Welfare, Shastri Bhavan, New Delhi.” 

[No. F. 10-1/71-T.6,] 
B. SEN, 

Dy, Educational Adviser (T).

MINISTRY OF INFORMATION AND BROADCASTING 

New Delhi, the 28th November 1971 

S.O. 278. — In exercise of the powers conferred by Rules 10 of the Cinemato- 
graph (Censorship) Rules, 1958, the Central Government has been pleased to 
appoint Shri D. Ramalingam as Assistant Regional Officer, in the office of the 
Central Board of Film Censors, Regional Centre, Madras, In a substantive capacity 
with effect from 1st October, 1971. 

[No. F. 2/6/69-FC.] 
C. B, GIRIDHAR, Dy. Secy.

MINISTRY OF HETROLKUM AND CHEMICAl^ 

(Department of Petroleum) 

New Delhi, the 27th November 1971 

8.0. 280. — ^Whereas it appears to the Central Government that it is necessary 
in the public interest that for the transport of petroleum from Well No. NKL 
to Kadi — I in Gujarat State, Pipelines should be laid by the Oil and 
Natural Gas Commission; 

And whereas it appears that for the purpose of laying such Pipelines, it is 
necessary to acquire the Right of User in the land described in the Schedule 
annexed hereto; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by sub-section (i) of the 
section 3 of the Petroleum Pipelines (Acquisition of Right of User in land) Act, 
1962 (50 of 1962), the Central Government hereby declares its intention to 
acquire the right of user therein: 

Provided that any person interested in the said land may, within 21 days from 
the date of this notification, object to the laying of the pipelines under the land 
to the Competent Authority, Oil and Natural Gas Commieeion, Construction and 
Maintenance Division, Makarpura Road, Baroda — 9, 

And every person making such an objection shall also state specifically whe- 
ther he wishes to be heard in person or by a legal practitioner.

New Delhi, the 30th November 1971 

S.O, 285. — Whereaa it appears to the Central Government that it is neces- 
sary in the public interest that for ground boding and wire laying from Bunk 
to ground bed for Cathodic protection to Collector line (near G.G.S. IV) Kalol 
oil Field in Gujarat State, Cathodic protection should be provided by the Oil 
and Natural Gas Commission. 

And whereas it appears that for the purpose of laying such ground beding and 
wire laying, it is necessary to acquire the Right of User in land described in the 
schedule annexed hereto; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by sub-section (i) of tho 
Section 3 of the Petroleum pipelines (Acquisition of Right of User in land) Act, 
1902 (50 of 1962), the Central Government hereby declares its intention to 
acquire the right of user therein; 

Provided that any person interested in the said land may, within 21 days from 
the date of this notification, object to the laying of the pipelines under the land to 
the (iompetent Authority, Oil and Natural Gas Commission, Construction and 
Maintenance Division, Makarpura Road, Baroda-9, 

And every person making such an objection shall state specifically whether 
he wises to be heard in person or by a legal practitioner.

S.O, 287. — Whereas it appears to the Central Government that it is necessary 
in the public interest that for the transport of petroleum products between the 
Haldia Port tDistrict-Midnapore) and Rajbandh Delivery Point (Distrlct-Burdwan) 
of Indian Oil Corporation Limited (Pipelines) in West Bengal State, pipelines 
should be laid by the Indian Oil Corporation Limited (Pipelines) and that for the 
purpose of laying such pipelines it is necessary to acquire the right of user in the 
land described in the schedule annexed hereto; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the pov^ers conferred 'bv Sub-Sectloh' (1) of Sec- 
tion 3 of the Petroleum Pipelines (Acquisition, of iRight of .User iij land) Act, 1962 
(50 of 1962) the Central Government hereby declare.s its inlention to acquire the 
right of user therein. 

Any person interested in the said land may within' 21 days from the date of this- 
Nodncalion obiect to the laying ot pipelines under the land, to Competent Authority 
at 9, Syed Amir All Avenue {3rd Floor), Calcutta-17, in the Office of the Indian Oil 
Corporation Limited (Pipelines). Every person making such an objection shall also 
state specifically whether he wishes to be heard in person or by legal practitioner.

S.O. 289. — ^Whereas it appears to the Central Government that it i,s necessary in 
the public interest that for the transport of petroleum products between the Haldia 
Port (District-Midnapore) and Rajbandh Delivery Point (District— Burdwan) of 
Indian Oil Corporation Limited (Pipelines) in West Bengal State, pipelines should 
bo laid by the Indian Oil Corporation Limited (Pipelines) and that for the purpose 
ot laying such pipellne.s it is nece.ssary to acquire the right of user in the land 
described in the schedu e annexed hereto; 

NeW', therefore, in exercise ot the powens conferred by Sub-Section (1) of Sec- 
tion 3 of the Petroleum Pipelines (Acquisition of Right of User in Umd) Act, 1962 
(,60 of 1962) the Central Government hereby declares its intention to acquire the 
right of u.ser therein. 

Any pcr.son interested in the .said land may within 21 days from the date of this 
NotificuKon object to the laying of pipelines under the land, to Competent Authority 
at 9, Syed Amir Ali Avenue (.Ird Floor)^ Calcutla-17, In the offlrc of the Indian Oil 
Corpfiration Limited (Pipelines). Every person making such an objection shall also 
state specifically whether he wishes to be heard in person or by legal practitioner.

MINISTRY OF STEEL AND MINES 
(Department of Mines) 

New Delhi, the 18tl» December 1971 

S.O, 290.— Whereas by the notification of the Government of India in the late 
Ministry of Petroleum and Chemicals and Mines and Mcta’.s (Department of Mines 
and Metals) No. S.O. 1616 dated the 13ih April, 1970, under sub-section (1) of Ac- 
tion 7 of the Coal Bearing Areas (Acquisition and Development) Act, 1967 (20 of 
1967), the Central Government gave notice of its intention to acquire the lands and 
mining rights In the locality specified in the Schedule appended to that notification; 

And whereas the competent authority In pursuance of section 8 of the said Act 
has made his rejjort to the Central Government; 

And whereas the Central Government, after considering the said report and after 
consulting the Government of Madhya Pradesh, is satisfied that: — 

(a) the lands measuring 81.20 acres (approximately) or 32,80 hectares (ap- 

proximately), described In Schedule ‘A’ appended hereto; and 

(b) the rights to mine, quarry, bore, dig and search for, win, work and carry 

away minerals in the lands measuring 422.00 acres (approximately) or 
170.91 hectares (approximately) described In Schedule ‘B’ appended 
hereto, 

should be acquired; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by sub-section (1) of section 
9 of the said Act, the Central Government hereby declares that the lands measuring 
81.20 acres (approximately) or 32.86 hectares (approximately) described in the said 
Schedule ’A’ and the rights to mine, quarry, bore, dig and search for, win, work, 
and carry away minerals in the lands measuring 422.00 acres (approximately) or 
170.91 hectares (approximately), described In the said Schedule ‘B’ are hereb.v 
acquired. 

The Plans of the area covered by 1his notification may be Inspected in; the Office 
of the Collector, Betul (Madhya Pradesh), or in the Office of the Coal Controller, 
1 Council House Street, Calcut''a or in the office of the National Coal Development 
Corporation Limited (Revenue Section). Darbhanga House, Ranchi,

ALUGABH MUSLIM UNIVERSITY 
STATEMENT OF ACCOUNTS, 1967-68 AND AUDIT REPORT 


S.O. 291 .— 


Audit Report or. the Accounts of the Aligarh Muslim University, Aligarh for the 

Year 1967-68 


1. Polytechnic for Girls — 

On the recommendation of the All-India Council lor Technical Education, a 
three-year diploma course was started for girls In October, 1966 in a polytechnic. 
The expenditure was to be met from out of grants by the University Grants 
Commission. The courses were electronics engineering, architectural assistant- 
ship and costume design dress-making. An annual In-take of 20 students in each 
course was contemplated. The admissions to the first two courses were as 
follows: — 


Year 

No. admitted in 
electronics engineering 
first year 

No. admitted ini 
architectural assistantship 
first year 

1966-67 

8 

19 

1967-68 

5 

4 

1968-69 

6 

•• 


The admission in the two courses was far below the estimated annual in-take. 

In October, 1967, the Principal, University Polytechnic suggested, because 
of the poor intake abolition of the course of architectural assistantship. It was 
abolished in July 1968 and Instead, a two-year diploma course in office assistant- 
ship and secretarial practice was started iAugust, 1968) and 13 trainees were 
admitted to it. Expenditure for this course is being met from the allotment 
sanctioned for architectural assistantship. In May, 1968, the University applied 
to the University Grants Commission for approval of the change. The approval 
is still awaited (August, 1969). 

2, University Brick Kiln — 

The University owns a brick kiln which is run through a contractor. Accord- 
ing to the terms and conditions of the contract entered into for three years (1966- 
66 to 1967-68), for manufacture and supply of bricks the University was to pro- 
vide to the contractor coal @ 22 tonnes per lakh of bricks In the first year and 
@ 20 tonnes per lakh in the subsequent years, provided that if the consumption 
of coal exceeded this limit the cost of such excess upto 2 tonnes of coal con- 
sumed was to be recovered at Rs. 64 per tonne and if the consumption went up 
still further the rate of recovery was to be Rs. 89 per tonne on the entire excess 
quantity of coal consumed. During 1965-66 to 1967-68, the University supplied 
408.10 tonnes of coal in excess of the quantity required to be supplied according 
to the contract. The cost at the above rates (Rs. 36.321) was still fo be recovered 
from the contractor (August, 1969). 

Against the minimum stipulated quantity of 60 lakh bricks, the contractor 
supplied only 30.77 lakh bricks during the period of the contract. Because of the 
short supply, the University had to purchase 3 lakh first class bricks at Rs. 65 per 
thousand and 4.7 lakh second class bricks at Rs. 61 per thousand from the open 
market during 1967-68. The issue rate of bricks from the kiln to the University 
was Rs. 36.50 per thousand for first class bricks and Rs. 33.60 per thousand for 
second class bricks. On this basis, the additional expenditure incurred on the 
purchase of bricks from the open market was Rs. 21,775 (including carriage 
charges). According to the terms of the contract, the contractor wa.s to pay to 
the University the entire loss and damage caused by non-fulfilment of the condi- 
tions of the contract. It has been stated by tiie University (August, 1969) that the 
question of Imposition of penalty for the .short supply is under consideration. 

3. Loss of Revenue — 

For the Medical College 79 residences (for professors, readers and lecturers) 
costing Rs, 28.83 iakhs.and 112 quarters (for ministerial .staff) costing Rs. 15.83 
lakhs were got constructed through a contractor and were taken over by the 
University on 28th February, 1968 and 31 July, respectively. Owing to non- 
availability of water supply, electric installations and approach roads, these 
residences and quarters have not so far (August. 1969) been let out. It was 
stated (November, 1968) by the University authorities that the above works could 
not be taken up along with the civil works owing to lack of proper co-ordination 
by the architect (a private person) and non-availability of funds. The loss of 
rent (till August, 1969 at the rate of 10 per cent of minimum of the pay scales 
of these for whom the quarters were constructed) is Rs. 1.48 lakhs approximately. 

Also Rs. 0.28 lakh have been spent (upto July, 1989) on the pay and allow- 
ances of 12 chowkidars for the buildings. 



Sl;c. 3 (ii)] THE GAZETIE OF INDIA; JANUARY 1, 1972/PAUSA 11, 1893 367 


4, Improper Maintenance of Cash Receipt Books — 

Acc&rding to paragraph 166 of the Manual of Office Procedure of the Finance 
and Accounts Department, 1968 all receipt books are to be got printed by the 
Accounts Officer of the Central Accounts Office, kept in his personal custody and 
treated as classified documents. This regulation has, howevei:, not been strictly 
followed as many departments/colleges of the University (a few mentioned be- 
low) have themselves got printed their own receipt books and brought them into 
use without obtaining the- receipt books from the Central Accounts Office: — 

1, Medical College 

2. Tibbiya College 

3. Building Department 

4, Estate Office 

6. Agriculture Department 

0, Horticulture Department 

7. Proctor's Office 

8. Gust House 

9. Library 

10. Botany Department 

11. Engineering College Workshop, etc. 

In November, 1968, the Treasurer issued a directive to all the departments, 
colleges/schools for stopping use of their own receipt books and obtaining their 
requirements from the Central Accounts Office only. These instructions have 
not been implemented (August, 1969). 

Also, there was no periodical verification of the total number of receipt books. 
Lack of control over printing, custody and distribution of receipt books and 
absence of periodical verification thereof can facilitate misappropriation of Uni- 
versity receipts.

AUDIT CERTIFICATE 

I have examined the loregoinK accounts and the Balance Sheet of the Aligarh 
Muslim University^ ,^Jlgarh, and obtained aU. the information and explanations 
that I have required and subject to the observations in the separate Audit Report, 
1 certify, aa a result of my audit, that In my opinion these accounts and the 
Balance Sheet are pjoperly drawn up so as to exhibit a true and fair view of 
the state of affairs of the Aligarh Muslim University, Aligarh, according to the 
best of my information and explanations given to me and as shown by the books 
of the Aligarh Muslim University, Allgarn. 

AUahabad: N. K. BHATTACHARYYA, 

The 3rd October, 1960, Accountant General, Uttar Pradesh. 

Countersigned. 

New Dellii: S. RANGANATHAN, 

The 9th October, 1960. Comptroller and Auditor General of India.
